Works with local, ssh, docker, etc.\n272\t#\n273\t# SECURITY WARNING: Password stored in plaintext!\n274\t#\n275\t# INTERACTIVE PROMPT: If sudo_password is unset and the CLI is running,\n276\t# you'll be prompted to enter your password when sudo is needed:\n277\t# - 45-second timeout (auto-skips if no input)\n278\t# - Press Enter to skip (command fails gracefully)\n279\t# - Password is hidden while typing\n280\t# - Password is cached for the session\n281\t#\n282\t# EMPTY PASSWORDS: Setting sudo_password to an explicit empty string is different\n283\t# from leaving it unset. Hermes will try an empty password via `sudo -S` and\n284\t# will not open the interactive prompt. This is useful for passwordless sudo,\n285\t# Touch ID sudo setups, and environments where prompting is just noise.\n286\t#\n287\t# ALTERNATIVES:\n288\t# - SSH backend: Configure passwordless sudo on the remote server\n289\t# - Containers: Run as root inside the container (no sudo needed)\n290\t# - Local: Configure /etc/sudoers for specific commands\n291\t#\n292\t# Example (add to your terminal section):\n293\t#   sudo_password: \"your-password-here\"\n294\t\n295\t# =============================================================================\n296\t# Security Scanning (tirith)\n297\t# =============================================================================\n298\t# Optional pre-exec command security scanning via tirith.\n299\t# Detects homograph URLs, pipe-to-shell, terminal injection, env manipulation.\n300\t# Install: brew install sheeki03/tap/tirith\n301\t# Docs: https://github.com/sheeki03/tirith\n302\t#\n303\t# security:\n304\t#   tirith_enabled: true        # Enable/disable tirith scanning\n305\t#   tirith_path: \"tirith\"       # Path to tirith binary (supports ~ expansion)\n306\t#   tirith_timeout: 5           # Scan timeout in seconds\n307\t#   tirith_fail_open: true      # Allow commands if tirith unavailable\n308\t\n309\t# =============================================================================\n310\t# Browser Tool Configuration\n311\t# =============================================================================\n312\tbrowser:\n313\t  # Inactivity timeout in seconds - browser sessions are automatically closed\n314\t  # after this period of no activity between agent loops (default: 120 = 2 minutes)\n315\t  inactivity_timeout: 120\n316\t\n317\t# =============================================================================\n318\t# Tool Loop Guardrails\n319\t# =============================================================================\n320\t# Soft warnings are enabled by default. They append guidance to repeated failed\n321\t# or non-progressing tool results but still let the tool execute. Hard stops are\n322\t# opt-in circuit breakers for autonomous/cron sessions where stopping a loop is\n323\t# preferable to spending the full iteration budget.\n324\ttool_loop_guardrails:\n325\t  warnings_enabled: true\n326\t  hard_stop_enabled: false\n327\t  warn_after:\n328\t    exact_failure: 2\n329\t    same_tool_failure: 3\n330\t    idempotent_no_progress: 2\n331\t  hard_stop_after:\n332\t    exact_failure: 5\n333\t    same_tool_failure: 8\n334\t    idempotent_no_progress: 5\n335\t\n336\t# =============================================================================\n337\t# Context Compression (Auto-shrinks long conversations)\n338\t# =============================================================================\n339\t# When conversation approaches model's context limit, middle turns are\n340\t# automatically summarized to free up space while preserving important context.\n341\t#\n342\t# HOW IT WORKS:\n343\t# 1. Tracks actual token usage from API responses (not estimates)\n344\t# 2. When prompt_tokens >= threshold% of model's context_length, triggers compression\n345\t# 3. Protects first 3 turns (system prompt, initial request, first response)\n346\t# 4. Protects last N turns (default 20 messages = ~10 full turns of recent context)\n347\t# 5. Summarizes middle turns using a fast/cheap model\n348\t# 6. Inserts summary as a user message, continues conversation seamlessly\n349\t#\n350\t# Post-compression tail budget is target_ratio × threshold × context_length:\n351\t#   200K context, threshold 0.50, ratio 0.20 → 20K tokens of recent tail preserved\n352\t#   1M   context, threshold 0.50, ratio 0.20 → 100K tokens of recent tail preserved\n353\t#\n354\tcompression:\n355\t  # Enable automatic context compression (default: true)\n356\t  # Set to false if you prefer to manage context manually or want errors on overflow\n357\t  enabled: true\n358\t  \n359\t  # Trigger compression at this % of model's context limit (default: 0.50 = 50%)\n360\t  # Lower values = more aggressive compression, higher values = compress later\n361\t  threshold: 0.50\n362\t  \n363\t  # Fraction of the threshold to preserve as recent tail (default: 0.20 = 20%)\n364\t  # e.g. 20% of 50% threshold = 10% of total context kept as recent messages.\n365\t  # Summary output is separately capped at 12K tokens (Gemini output limit).\n366\t  # Range: 0.10 - 0.80\n367\t  target_ratio: 0.20\n368\t\n369\t  # Number of most-recent messages to always preserve (default: 20 ≈ 10 full turns)\n370\t  # Higher values keep more recent conversation intact at the cost of more aggressive\n371\t  # compression of older turns.\n372\t  protect_last_n: 20\n373\t\n374\t  # Number of non-system messages to protect at the head of the transcript, in\n375\t  # ADDITION to the system prompt (which is always implicitly protected).\n376\t  # Head messages are NEVER summarized — they survive every compression\n377\t  # indefinitely. This gives stable early context for short/medium sessions,\n378\t  # but in long-running sessions that rely on rolling compaction the pinned\n379\t  # opening turns may not match how you want the session framed over time.\n380\t  # Set to 0 to preserve ONLY the system prompt (plus the rolling summary\n381\t  # and recent tail) — the cleanest configuration for long-running sessions.\n382\t  # Default 3 preserves the system prompt plus the first three non-system\n383\t  # head messages, matching the pre-feature behaviour.\n384\t  protect_first_n: 3\n385\t\n386\t  # To pin a specific model/provider for compression summaries, use the\n387\t  # auxiliary section below (auxiliary.compression.provider / model).\n388\t\n389\t# =============================================================================\n390\t# Anthropic prompt caching TTL\n391\t# =============================================================================\n392\t# When prompt caching is active (Claude via OpenRouter or native Anthropic),\n393\t# Anthropic supports two TTL tiers for cached prefixes: \"5m\" (default) and\n394\t# \"1h\". Other values are ignored and \"5m\" is used.\n395\t#\n396\tprompt_caching:\n397\t  cache_ttl: \"5m\" # use \"1h\" for long sessions with pauses between turns\n398\t\n399\t# =============================================================================\n400\t# Auxiliary Models (Advanced — Experimental)\n401\t# =============================================================================\n402\t# Hermes uses lightweight \"auxiliary\" models for side tasks: image analysis,\n403\t# browser screenshot analysis, web page summarization, and context compression.\n404\t#\n405\t# By default these use Gemini Flash via OpenRouter or Nous Portal and are\n406\t# auto-detected from your credentials.  You do NOT need to change anything\n407\t# here for normal usage.\n408\t#\n409\t# WARNING: Overriding these with providers other than OpenRouter or Nous Portal\n410\t# is EXPERIMENTAL and may not work.  Not all models/providers support vision,\n411\t# produce usable summaries, or accept the same API format.  Change at your own\n412\t# risk — if things break, reset to \"auto\" / empty values.\n413\t#\n414\t# Each task has its own provider + model pair so you can mix providers.\n415\t# For example: OpenRouter for vision (needs multimodal), but your main\n416\t# local endpoint for compression (just needs text).\n417\t#\n418\t# Provider options:\n419\t#   \"auto\"       - Best available: OpenRouter → Nous Portal → main endpoint (default)\n420\t#   \"openrouter\" - Force OpenRouter (requires OPENROUTER_API_KEY)\n421\t#   \"nous\"       - Force Nous Portal (requires: hermes login)\n422\t#   \"gemini\"      - Force Google AI Studio direct (requires: GOOGLE_API_KEY or GEMINI_API_KEY)\n423\t#   \"ollama-cloud\" - Ollama Cloud (requires: OLLAMA_API_KEY)\n424\t#   \"codex\"       - Force Codex OAuth (requires: hermes model → Codex).\n425\t#                  Uses gpt-5.3-codex which supports vision.\n426\t#   \"main\"       - Use your custom endpoint (OPENAI_BASE_URL + OPENAI_API_KEY).\n427\t#                  Works with OpenAI API, local models, or any OpenAI-compatible\n428\t#                  endpoint.  Also falls back to Codex OAuth and API-key providers.\n429\t#\n430\t# Model: leave empty to use the provider's default.  When empty, OpenRouter\n431\t# uses \"google/gemini-3-flash-preview\" and Nous uses \"gemini-3-flash\".\n432\t# Other providers pick a sensible default automatically.\n433\t#\n434\t# auxiliary:\n435\t#   # Image analysis: vision_analyze tool + browser screenshots\n436\t#   vision:\n437\t#     provider: \"auto\"\n438\t#     model: \"\"              # e.g. \"google/gemini-2.5-flash\", \"openai/gpt-4o\"\n439\t#     timeout: 30            # LLM API call timeout (seconds)\n440\t#     download_timeout: 30   # Image HTTP download timeout (seconds)\n441\t#                            # Increase for slow connections or self-hosted image servers\n442\t#\n443\t#   # Web page scraping / summarization + browser page text extraction\n444\t#   web_extract:\n445\t#     provider: \"auto\"\n446\t#     model: \"\"\n447\t#\n448\t#   # Session search — summarizes matching past sessions\n449\t#   session_search:\n450\t#     provider: \"auto\"\n451\t#     model: \"\"\n452\t#     timeout: 30\n453\t#     max_concurrency: 3    # Limit parallel summaries to reduce request-burst 429s\n454\t#     extra_body: {}        # Provider-specific OpenAI-compatible request fields\n455\t#                           # Example for providers that support request-body\n456\t#                           # reasoning controls:\n457\t#                           # extra_body:\n458\t#                           #   enable_thinking: false\n459\t\n460\t# =============================================================================\n461\t# Persistent Memory\n462\t# =============================================================================\n463\t# Bounded curated memory injected into the system prompt every session.\n464\t# Two stores: MEMORY.md (agent's notes) and USER.md (user profile).\n465\t# Character limits keep the memory small and focused. The agent manages\n466\t# pruning -- when at the limit, it must consolidate or replace entries.\n467\t# Disabled by default in batch_runner.\n468\t#\n469\tmemory:\n470\t  # Agent's personal notes: environment facts, conventions, things learned\n471\t  memory_enabled: true\n472\t  \n473\t  # User profile: preferences, communication style, expectations\n474\t  user_profile_enabled: true\n475\t  \n476\t  # Character limits (~2.75 chars per token, model-independent)\n477\t  memory_char_limit: 2200   # ~800 tokens\n478\t  user_char_limit: 1375     # ~500 tokens\n479\t\n480\t  # Periodic memory nudge: remind the agent to consider saving memories\n481\t  # every N user turns. Set to 0 to disable. Only active when memory is enabled.\n482\t  nudge_interval: 10        # Nudge every 10 user turns (0 = disabled)\n483\t\n484\t  # Memory flush: give the agent one turn to save memories before context is\n485\t  # lost (compression, /new, /reset, exit). Set to 0 to disable.\n486\t  # For exit/reset, only fires if the session had at least this many user turns.\n487\t  flush_min_turns: 6        # Min user turns to trigger flush on exit/reset (0 = disabled)\n488\t\n489\t# =============================================================================\n490\t# Session Reset Policy (Messaging Platforms)\n491\t# =============================================================================\n492\t# Controls when messaging sessions (Telegram, Discord, WhatsApp, Slack) are\n493\t# automatically cleared. Without resets, conversation context grows indefinitely\n494\t# which increases API costs with every message.\n495\t#\n496\t# When a reset triggers, the agent first saves important information to its\n497\t# persistent memory — but the conversation context is wiped. The agent starts\n498\t# fresh but retains learned facts via its memory system.\n499\t#\n500\t# Users can always manually reset with /reset or /new in chat.\n501\t#\n502\t# Modes:\n503\t#   \"both\"  - Reset on EITHER inactivity timeout or daily boundary (recommended)\n504\t#   \"idle\"  - Reset only after N minutes of inactivity\n505\t#   \"daily\" - Reset only at a fixed hour each day\n506\t#   \"none\"  - Never auto-reset; context lives until /reset or compression kicks in\n507\t#\n508\t# When a reset triggers, the agent gets one turn to save important memories and\n509\t# skills before the context is wiped. Persistent memory carries across sessions.\n510\t#\n511\tsession_reset:\n512\t  mode: both           # \"both\", \"idle\", \"daily\", or \"none\"\n513\t  idle_minutes: 1440   # Inactivity timeout in minutes (default: 1440 = 24 hours)\n514\t  at_hour: 4           # Daily reset hour, 0-23 local time (default: 4 AM)\n515\t\n516\t# When true, group/channel chats use one session per participant when the platform\n517\t# provides a user ID. This is the secure default and prevents users in the same\n518\t# room from sharing context, interrupts, and token costs. Set false only if you\n519\t# explicitly want one shared \"room brain\" per group/channel.\n520\tgroup_sessions_per_user: true\n521\t\n522\t# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n523\t# Gateway Streaming\n524\t# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n525\t# Stream tokens to messaging platforms in real-time. The bot sends a message\n526\t# on first token, then progressively edits it as more tokens arrive.\n527\t# Disabled by default — enable to try the streaming UX on Telegram/Discord/Slack.\n528\t# For Telegram, partial edits are sent as plain text and only the final edit uses MarkdownV2.\n529\tstreaming:\n530\t  enabled: false\n531\t  # transport: edit           # \"edit\" = progressive editMessageText\n532\t  # edit_interval: 0.3        # seconds between message edits\n533\t  # buffer_threshold: 40      # chars before forcing an edit flush\n534\t  # cursor: \" ▉\"              # cursor shown during streaming\n535\t\n536\t# =============================================================================\n537\t# Skills Configuration\n538\t# =============================================================================\n539\t# Skills are reusable procedures the agent can load and follow. The agent can\n540\t# also create new skills after completing complex tasks.\n541\t#\n542\tskills:\n543\t  # Nudge the agent to create skills after complex tasks.\n544\t  # Every N tool-calling iterations, remind the model to consider saving a skill.\n545\t  # Set to 0 to disable.\n546\t  creation_nudge_interval: 15\n547\t\n548\t  # External skill directories — share skills across tools/agents without\n549\t  # copying them into ~/.hermes/skills/.  Each path is expanded (~ and ${VAR})\n550\t  # and resolved to an absolute path.  External dirs are read-only: skill\n551\t  # creation always writes to ~/.hermes/skills/.  Local skills take precedence\n552\t  # when names collide.\n553\t  # external_dirs:\n554\t  #   - ~/.agents/skills\n555\t  #   - /home/shared/team-skills\n556\t\n557\t# =============================================================================\n558\t# Agent Behavior\n559\t# =============================================================================\n560\tagent:\n561\t  # Maximum tool-calling iterations per conversation\n562\t  # Higher = more room for complex tasks, but costs more tokens\n563\t  # Recommended: 20-30 for focused tasks, 50-100 for open exploration\n564\t  max_turns: 60\n565\t\n566\t  # Inactivity timeout for gateway agent runs (seconds, 0 = unlimited).\n567\t  # The agent can run indefinitely when actively calling tools or receiving\n568\t  # API responses.  Only fires after the agent has been idle for this duration.\n569\t  # gateway_timeout: 1800\n570\t\n571\t  # Staged warning: send a warning before escalating to full timeout.\n572\t  # Fires once per run when inactivity reaches this threshold (seconds).\n573\t  # Set to 0 to disable the warning.\n574\t  # gateway_timeout_warning: 900\n575\t\n576\t  # Graceful drain timeout for gateway stop/restart (seconds).\n577\t  # The gateway stops accepting new work, waits for in-flight agents to\n578\t  # finish, then interrupts anything still running after this timeout.\n579\t  # 0 = no drain, interrupt immediately.\n580\t  # restart_drain_timeout: 60\n581\t\n582\t  # Max app-level retry attempts for API errors (connection drops, provider\n583\t  # timeouts, 5xx, etc.) before the agent surfaces the failure. Lower this\n584\t  # to 1 if you use fallback providers and want fast failover on flaky\n585\t  # primaries (default 3). The OpenAI SDK does its own low-level retries\n586\t  # underneath this wrapper — this is the Hermes-level loop.\n587\t  # api_max_retries: 3\n588\t  \n589\t  # Enable verbose logging\n590\t  verbose: false\n591\t  \n592\t  # Reasoning effort level (OpenRouter and Nous Portal)\n593\t  # Controls how much \"thinking\" the model does before responding.\n594\t  # Options: \"xhigh\" (max), \"high\", \"medium\", \"low\", \"minimal\", \"none\" (disable)\n595\t  reasoning_effort: \"medium\"\n596\t  \n597\t  # Predefined personalities (use with /personality command)\n598\t  personalities:\n599\t    helpful: \"You are a helpful, friendly AI assistant.\"\n600\t    concise: \"You are a concise assistant. Keep responses brief and to the point.\"\n601\t    technical: \"You are a technical expert. Provide detailed, accurate technical information.\"\n602\t    creative: \"You are a creative assistant.      - web_search only (no scraping)\n712\t#   terminal     - terminal, process\n713\t#   file         - read_file, write_file, patch, search\n714\t#   browser      - browser_navigate, Tracks actual token usage from API responses (not estimates)\n# 2. When prompt_tokens >= threshold% of model's context_length, triggers compression\n# 3. Protects first 3 turns (system prompt, initial request, first response)\n# 4. Protects last N turns (default 20 messages = ~10 full turns of recent context)\n# 5. Summarizes middle turns using a fast/cheap model\n# 6. Inserts summary as a user message, continues conversation seamlessly\n#\n# Post-compression tail budget is target_ratio × threshold × context_length:\n#   200K context, threshold 0.50, ratio 0.20 → 20K tokens of recent tail preserved\n#   1M   context, threshold 0.50, ratio 0.20 → 100K tokens of recent tail preserved\n#\ncompression:\n  # Enable automatic context compression (default: true)\n  # Set to false if you prefer to manage context manually or want errors on overflow\n  enabled: true\n  \n  # Trigger compression at this % of model's context limit (default: 0.50 = 50%)\n  # Lower values = more aggressive compression, higher values = compress later\n  threshold: 0.50\n  \n  # Fraction of the threshold to preserve as recent tail (default: 0.20 = 20%)\n  # e.g. 20% of 50% threshold = 10% of total context kept as recent messages.\n  # Summary output is separately capped at 12K tokens (Gemini output limit).\n  # Range: 0.10 - 0.80\n  target_ratio: 0.20\n\n  # Number of most-recent messages to always preserve (default: 20 ≈ 10 full turns)\n  # Higher values keep more recent conversation intact at the cost of more aggressive\n  # compression of older turns.\n  protect_last_n: 20\n\n  # Number of non-system messages to protect at the head of the transcript, in\n  # ADDITION to the system prompt (which is always implicitly protected).\n  # Head messages are NEVER summarized — they survive every compression\n  # indefinitely. This gives stable early context for short/medium sessions,\n  # but in long-running sessions that rely on rolling compaction the pinned\n  # opening turns may not match how you want the session framed over time.\n  # Set to 0 to preserve ONLY the system prompt (plus the rolling summary\n  # and recent tail) — the cleanest configuration for long-running sessions.\n  # Default 3 preserves the system prompt plus the first three non-system\n  # head messages, matching the pre-feature behaviour.\n  protect_first_n: 3\n\n  # To pin a specific model/provider for compression summaries, use the\n  # auxiliary section below (auxiliary.compression.provider / model).\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Anthropic prompt caching TTL\n# =============================================================================\n# When prompt caching is active (Claude via OpenRouter or native Anthropic),\n# Anthropic supports two TTL tiers for cached prefixes: \"5m\" (default) and\n# \"1h\". The agent manages\n# pruning -- when at the limit, it must consolidate or replace entries.\n# Disabled by default in batch_runner.\n#\nmemory:\n  # Agent's personal notes: environment facts, conventions, things learned\n  memory_enabled: true\n  \n  # User profile: preferences, communication style, expectations\n  user_profile_enabled: true\n  \n  # Character limits (~2.75 chars per token, model-independent)\n  memory_char_limit: 2200   # ~800 tokens\n  user_char_limit: 1375     # ~500 tokens\n\n  # Periodic memory nudge: remind the agent to consider saving memories\n  # every N user turns. Set to 0 to disable.\n  # For exit/reset, only fires if the session had at least this many user turns.\n  flush_min_turns: 6        # Min user turns to trigger flush on exit/reset (0 = disabled)\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Session Reset Policy (Messaging Platforms)\n# =============================================================================\n# Controls when messaging sessions (Telegram, Discord, WhatsApp, Slack) are\n# automatically cleared. Without resets, conversation context grows indefinitely\n# which increases API costs with every message.\n#\n# When a reset triggers, the agent first saves important information to its\n# persistent memory — but the conversation context is wiped. The agent starts\n# fresh but retains learned facts via its memory system.\n#\n# Users can always manually reset with /reset or /new in chat.\n#\n# Modes:\n#   \"both\"  - Reset on EITHER inactivity timeout or daily boundary (recommended)\n#   \"idle\"  - Reset only after N minutes of inactivity\n#   \"daily\" - Reset only at a fixed hour each day\n#   \"none\"  - Never auto-reset; context lives until /reset or compression kicks in\n#\n# When a reset triggers, the agent gets one turn to save important memories and\n# skills before the context is wiped. Persistent memory carries across sessions.\n#\nsession_reset:\n  mode: both           # \"both\", \"idle\", \"daily\", or \"none\"\n  idle_minutes: 1440   # Inactivity timeout in minutes (default: 1440 = 24 hours)\n  at_hour: 4           # Daily reset hour, 0-23 local time (default: 4 AM)\n\n# When true, group/channel chats use one session per participant when the platform\n# provides a user ID. This is the secure default and prevents users in the same\n# room from sharing context, interrupts, and token costs. Each path is expanded (~ and ${VAR})\n  # and resolved to an absolute path.  External dirs are read-only: skill\n  # creation always writes to ~/.hermes/skills/.  Local skills take precedence\n  # when names collide.\n  # external_dirs:\n  #   - ~/.agents/skills\n  #   - /home/shared/team-skills\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Agent Behavior\n# =============================================================================\nagent:\n  # Maximum tool-calling iterations per conversation\n  # Higher = more room for complex tasks, but costs more tokens\n  # Recommended: 20-30 for focused tasks, 50-100 for open exploration\n  max_turns: 60\n\n  # Inactivity timeout for gateway agent runs (seconds, 0 = unlimited).\n  # The agent can run indefinitely when actively calling tools or receiving\n  # API responses.  Only fires after the agent has been idle for this duration.\n  # gateway_timeout: 1800\n\n  # Staged warning: send a warning before escalating to full timeout.\n  # Fires once per run when inactivity reaches this threshold (seconds).\n  # Set to 0 to disable the warning.\n  # gateway_timeout_warning: 900\n\n  # Graceful drain timeout for gateway stop/restart (seconds).\n  # The gateway stops accepting new work, waits for in-flight agents to\n  # finish, then interrupts anything still running after this timeout.\n  # 0 = no drain, interrupt immediately.\n  # restart_drain_timeout: 60\n\n  # Max app-level retry attempts for API errors (connection drops, provider\n  # timeouts, 5xx, etc.) before the agent surfaces the failure. Lower this\n  # to 1 if you use fallback providers and want fast failover on flaky\n  # primaries (default 3). The OpenAI SDK does its own low-level retries\n  # underneath this wrapper — this is the Hermes-level loop.\n  # api_max_retries: 3\n  \n  # Enable verbose logging\n  verbose: false\n  \n  # Reasoning effort level (OpenRouter and Nous Portal)\n  # Controls how much \"thinking\" the model does before responding.\n  # Options: \"xhigh\" (max), \"high\", \"medium\", \"low\", \"minimal\", \"none\" (disable)\n  reasoning_effort: \"medium\"\n  \n  # Predefined personalities (use with /personality command)\n  personalities:\n    helpful: \"You are a helpful, friendly AI assistant.\"\n    concise: \"You are a concise assistant. Keep responses brief and to the point.\"\n    technical: \"You are a technical expert. Provide detailed, accurate technical information.\"\n    creative: \"You are a creative assistant. Think outside the box and offer innovative solutions.\"\n    teacher: \"You are a patient teacher. Explain concepts clearly with examples.\"\n    kawaii: \"You are a kawaii assistant! LET'S DO THIS! 💪😤🚀\"\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Toolsets\n# =============================================================================\n# platform keys: cli, telegram, discord, whatsapp, slack, qqbot, teams, google_chat\n#\n# Examples:\n#\n#   # Use presets (same as defaults):\n#   platform_toolsets:\n#     cli: [hermes-cli]\n# =============================================================================\n# MCP (Model Context Protocol) Servers\n# =============================================================================\n# Connect to external MCP servers to add tools from the MCP ecosystem.\n# Each server's tools are automatically discovered and registered.\n# See docs/mcp.md for full documentation.\n#\n# Stdio servers (spawn a subprocess):\n#   command: the executable to run\n#   args: command-line arguments\n#   env: environment variables (only these + safe defaults passed to subprocess)\n#\n# HTTP servers (connect to a URL):\n#   url: the MCP server endpoint\n#   headers: HTTP headers (e.g., for authentication)\n#\n# Optional per-server settings:\n#   timeout: tool call timeout in seconds (default: 120)\n#   connect_timeout: initial connection timeout (default: 60)\n#\n# mcp_servers:\n#   time:\n#     command: uvx\n#     args: [\"mcp-server-time\"]\n#   filesystem:\n#     command: npx\n#     args: [\"-y\", \"@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem\", \"/home/user\"]\n#   notion:\n#     url: https://mcp.notion.com/mcp\n#   github:\n#     command: npx\n#     args: [\"-y\", \"@modelcontextprotocol/server-github\"]\n#     env:\n#       GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN: \"ghp_...\"\n#\n# Sampling (server-initiated LLM requests) — enabled by default.\n# Per-server config under the 'sampling' key:\n#   analysis:\n#     command: npx\n#     args: [\"-y\", \"analysis-server\"]\n#     sampling:\n#       enabled: true           # default: true\n#       model: \"gemini-3-flash\" # override model (optional)\n#       max_tokens_cap: 4096    # max tokens per request\n#       timeout: 30             # LLM call timeout (seconds)\n#       max_rpm: 10             # max requests per minute\n#       allowed_models: []      # model whitelist (empty = all)\n#       max_tool_rounds: 5      # tool loop limit (0 = disable)\n#       log_level: \"info\"       # audit verbosity\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Voice Transcription (Speech-to-Text)\n# =============================================================================\n# Automatically transcribe voice messages on messaging platforms.\n# Providers: local (free, faster-whisper) | groq (free tier) | openai (Whisper API) | mistral (Voxtral Transcribe)\n# Set the corresponding API key in .env: GROQ_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, or MISTRAL_API_KEY.\nstt:\n  enabled: true\n  # provider: \"local\"          # auto-detected if omitted\n  local:\n    model: \"base\"              # tiny | base | small | medium | large-v3 | turbo\n    # language: \"\"             # \"voxtral-mini-latest\"  # voxtral-mini-latest | voxtral-mini-2602\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Response Pacing (Messaging Platforms)\n# =============================================================================\n# Add human-like delays between message chunks.\n# human_delay:\n#   mode: \"off\"      # \"off\" | \"natural\" | \"custom\"\n#   min_ms: 800      # Min delay (custom mode only)\n#   max_ms: 2500     # Max delay (custom mode only)\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Session Logging\n# =============================================================================\n# Session trajectories are automatically saved to logs/ directory.\n# Each session creates: logs/session_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS_UUID.json\n#\n# The session ID is displayed in the welcome banner for easy reference.\n# Logs contain full conversation history in trajectory format:\n# - System prompt, user messages, assistant responses\n# - Tool calls with inputs/outputs\n# - Timestamps for debugging\n#\n# No configuration needed - logging is always enabled.\n# To disable, you would need to modify the source code.\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Code Execution Sandbox (Programmatic Tool Calling)\n# =============================================================================\n# The execute_code tool runs Python scripts that call Hermes tools via RPC.\n# Intermediate tool results stay out of the LLM's context window.\ncode_execution:\n  timeout: 300         # Max seconds per script before kill (default: 300 = 5 min)\n  max_tool_calls: 50   # Max RPC tool calls per execution (default: 50)\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Subagent Delegation\n# =============================================================================\n# The delegate_task tool spawns child agents with isolated context.\n# Supports single tasks and batch mode (default 3 parallel, configurable).\ndelegation:\n  max_iterations: 50                          # Max tool-calling turns per child (default: 50)\n  # max_concurrent_children: 3                # Max parallel child agents per batch (default: 3, floor: 1, no ceiling).\n                                              # WARNING: values above 10 multiply API cost linearly.\n  # max_spawn_depth: 1                        # Delegation tree depth cap (range: 1-3, default: 1 = flat).\n                                              # Raise to 2 to allow workers to spawn their own subagents.\n                                              # Requires role=\"orchestrator\" on intermediate agents.\n  # orchestrator_enabled: true                # Kill switch for role=\"orchestrator\" children (default: true).\n  # subagent_auto_approve: false              # When a subagent hits a dangerous-command approval prompt, auto-deny (default: false)\n                                              # or auto-approve \"once\" (true) instead of blocking on stdin.\n                                              # The parent TUI owns stdin, so blocking would deadlock; non-interactive resolution is required.\n                                              # Both choices emit a logger.warning audit line. Flip to true only for cron/batch pipelines.\n  # inherit_mcp_toolsets: true                # When explicit child toolsets are narrowed, also keep the parent's MCP toolsets (default: true). Set false for strict intersection.\n  # model: \"google/gemini-3-flash-preview\"    # Override model for subagents (empty = inherit parent)\n  # provider: \"openrouter\"                    # Override provider for subagents (empty = inherit parent)\n  #                                           # Resolves full credentials (base_url, api_key) automatically.\n  #                                           # Supported: openrouter, nous, zai, kimi-coding, minimax\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Honcho Integration (Cross-Session User Modeling)\n# =============================================================================\n# AI-native persistent memory via Honcho (https://honcho.dev/).\n# Builds a deeper understanding of the user across sessions and tools.\n# Runs alongside USER.md — additive, not a replacement.\n#\n# Requires: pip install honcho-ai\n# Config: ~/.honcho/config.json (shared with Claude Code, Cursor, etc.)\n# API key: HONCHO_API_KEY in ~/.hermes/.env or ~/.honcho/config.json\n#\n# Hermes-specific overrides (optional — most config comes from ~/.honcho/config.json):\n# honcho: {}\n\n# =============================================================================\n# Display\n# =============================================================================\ndisplay:\n  # Use compact banner mode (hides the ASCII-art banner, shows a single line).\n  #   true:  Compact single-line banner\n  #   false: Full ASCII banner with tool/skill summary (default)\n  compact: false\n\n  # Tool progress display level (CLI and gateway)\n  #   off:     Silent — no tool activity shown, just the final response\n  #   new:     Show a tool indicator only when the tool changes (skip repeats)\n  #   all:     Show every tool call with a short preview (default)\n  #   verbose: Full args, results, and debug logs (same as /verbose)\n  # Toggle at runtime with /verbose in the CLI\n  tool_progress: all\n\n  # Per-platform defaults can be quieter than the global setting. Telegram\n  # tunes for mobile: tool_progress and busy_ack_detail default off (no\n  # per-tool breadcrumb stream, no \"iteration 21/60\" debug detail in busy\n  # acks or heartbeats), but interim_assistant_messages and\n  # long_running_notifications STAY ON so the user has real signal between\n  # turn start and final answer (mid-turn assistant commentary + a single\n  # edit-in-place \"⏳ Working — N min\" heartbeat). Override under\n  # display.platforms.telegram.\n\n  # Auto-cleanup of temporary progress bubbles after the final response lands.\n  # On platforms that support message deletion (currently Telegram), this\n  # removes the tool-progress bubble, \"⏳ Still working...\" notices, and\n  # context-pressure status messages once the final reply has been delivered —\n  # keeping long-running turns visible live, then tidy afterward. Failed runs\n  # leave the bubbles in place as breadcrumbs. Off by default.\n  # Per-platform override: display.platforms.telegram.cleanup_progress\n  #   true:  Delete tracked progress/status bubbles on successful turn\n  #   false: Leave everything in place (default)\n  # Example:\n  #   display:\n  #     platforms:\n  #       telegram:\n  #         cleanup_progress: true\n  cleanup_progress: false\n\n  # Gateway-only natural mid-turn assistant updates.\n  # When true, completed assistant status messages are sent as separate chat\n  # messages. This is independent of tool_progress and gateway streaming.\n  #   true:  Send mid-turn assistant updates as separate messages (default)\n  #   false: Only send the final response\n  interim_assistant_messages: true\n\n  # Gateway-only long-running status heartbeats.\n  # When false, the platform does not receive periodic \"⏳ Working — N min\"\n  # notifications even if agent.gateway_notify_interval is non-zero. The\n  # heartbeat edits a single message in place (where the adapter supports\n  # editing) instead of posting a new bubble each interval.\n  # Default: true everywhere, including Telegram (silent agents are worse\n  # than a single edit-in-place heartbeat).\n  long_running_notifications: true\n\n  # Include detailed iteration/tool/status context in busy acknowledgments\n  # and long-running heartbeats. When true, busy acks show \"iteration 21/60,\n  # terminal, 10 min\" and the heartbeat shows \"⏳ Working — 12 min,\n  # iteration 21/60, terminal\". When false (Telegram default), both stay\n  # terse: \"Interrupting current task\" and \"⏳ Working — 12 min, terminal\".\n  busy_ack_detail: true\n\n  # What Enter does when Hermes is already busy (CLI and gateway platforms).\n  #   interrupt: Interrupt the current run and redirect Hermes (default)\n  #   queue:     Queue your message for the next turn\n  #   steer:     Inject your message mid-run via /steer, arriving at the agent\n  #              after the next tool call — no interrupt, no role violation.\n  #              Falls back to 'queue' if the agent isn't running yet or if\n  #              images are attached (steer only carries text).\n  # Ctrl+C (or /stop in gateway) always interrupts regardless of this setting.\n  # Toggle at runtime with /busy <interrupt|queue|steer>.\n  busy_input_mode: interrupt\n\n  # Background process notifications (gateway/messaging only).\n  # Controls how chatty the process watcher is when you use\n  # terminal(background=true, notify_on_complete=true) from Telegram/Discord/etc.\n  #   off:     No watcher messages at all\n  #   result:  Only the final completion message\n  #   error:   Only the final message when exit code != 0\n  #   all:     Running output updates + final message (default)\n  background_process_notifications: all\n\n\n  # Play terminal bell when agent finishes a response.\n  # Useful for long-running tasks — your terminal will ding when the agent is done.\n  # Works over SSH. Most terminals can be configured to flash the taskbar or play a sound.\n  #   true:  Ring the terminal bell on each response\n  #   false: Silent (default)\n  bell_on_complete: false\n\n  # Show model reasoning/thinking before each response.\n  # When enabled, a dim box shows the model's thought process above the response.\n  # Toggle at runtime with /reasoning show or /reasoning hide.\n  #   true:  Show the reasoning box\n  #   false: Hide reasoning (default)\n  show_reasoning: false\n\n  # Stream tokens to the terminal as they arrive instead of waiting for the\n  # full response. The response box opens on first token and text appears\n  # line-by-line. Tool calls are still captured silently.\n  #   true:  Stream tokens as they arrive (default)\n  #   false: Wait for the full response before rendering\n  streaming: true\n\n  # Show [HH:MM] timestamps on user input and assistant response labels.\n  # timestamps: false\n\n  # ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  # Skin / Theme\n  # ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  # Customize CLI visual appearance — banner colors, spinner faces, tool prefix,\n  # response box label, and branding text. Change at runtime with /skin <name>.\n  #\n  # Built-in skins:\n  #   default        — Classic Hermes gold/kawaii\n  #   ares           — Crimson/bronze war-god theme with spinner wings\n  #   mono           — Clean grayscale monochrome\n  #   slate          — Cool blue developer-focused\n  #   daylight       — Bright light-mode theme\n  #   warm-lightmode — Warm paper-tone light-mode theme\n  #   poseidon       — Sea-green/teal Olympian theme\n  #   sisyphus       — Earthy stone-and-moss theme\n  #   charizard      — Fiery orange dragon theme\n  #\n  # Custom skins: drop a YAML file in ~/.hermes/skins/<name>.yaml\n  # Schema (all fields optional, missing values inherit from default):\n  #\n  #   name: my-theme\n  #   description: Short description\n  #   colors:\n  #     banner_border: \"#HEX\"    # Panel border\n  #     banner_title: \"#HEX\"     # Panel title\n  #     banner_accent: \"#HEX\"    # Section headers (Available Tools, etc.)\n  #     banner_dim: \"#HEX\"       # Dim/muted text\n  #     banner_text: \"#HEX\"      # Body text (tool names, skill names)\n  #     ui_accent: \"#HEX\"        # UI accent color\n  #     response_border: \"#HEX\"  # Response box border color\n  #   spinner:\n  #     waiting_faces: [\"(⚔)\", \"(⛨)\"]       # Faces shown while waiting\n  #     thinking_faces: [\"(⚔)\", \"(⌁)\"]      # Faces shown while thinking\n  #     thinking_verbs: [\"forging\", \"plotting\"]  # Verbs for spinner messages\n  #     wings:                                # Optional left/right spinner decorations\n  #       - [\"⟪⚔\", \"⚔⟫\"]\n  #       - [\"⟪▲\", \"▲⟫\"]\n  #   branding:\n  #     agent_name: \"My Agent\"               # Banner title and branding\n  #     welcome: \"Welcome message\"           # Shown at CLI startup\n  #     response_label: \" ⚔ Agent \"         # Response box header label\n  #     prompt_symbol: \"⚔\"                  # Prompt symbol (bare token;
